```tsx
import { useState } from 'preact/hooks'
import { signIn as signInWithCrewLuCloud } from '../auth/session'
import { SIGN_IN_MESSAGES, type SignInError } from '../auth/signInErrors'

export interface SignInProps {
  onSignedIn: () => void
  /** Injected in tests; defaults to the real CrewLu Cloud sign-in. */
  signIn?: (email: string, password: string) => Promise<SignInError | null>
}

/** The web-only sign-in screen (spec §4.2). A real form, so Enter submits and password managers fill it. */
export function SignIn({ onSignedIn, signIn = signInWithCrewLuCloud }: SignInProps) {
  const [email, setEmail] = useState('')
  const [password, setPassword] = useState('')
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null)
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false)

  const submit = async (event: Event) => {
    event.preventDefault()
    if (busy) return
    setBusy(true)
    setError(null)
    const failure = await signIn(email.trim(), password)
    setBusy(false)
    if (failure) setError(SIGN_IN_MESSAGES[failure])
    else onSignedIn()
  }

  return (
    <main class="screen signin">
      <div class="signin-mark">
        <img src="/icons/heart-256.png" alt="" width={88} height={88} />
      </div>
      <h1 class="title">CrewLuve</h1>
      <p class="signin-sub">Sign in with your pilot's CrewLu Cloud account</p>
      <form class="signin-form" onSubmit={submit}>
        <label class="sr-only" for="email">Email</label>
        <input
          id="email"
          class="field"
          type="email"
          autocomplete="username"
          placeholder="pilot@example.com"
          required
          value={email}
          onInput={(e) => setEmail(e.currentTarget.value)}
        />
        <label class="sr-only" for="password">Password</label>
        <input
          id="password"
          class="field"
          type="password"
          autocomplete="current-password"
          placeholder="Password"
          required
          value={password}
          onInput={(e) => setPassword(e.currentTarget.value)}
        />
        <button class="primary" type="submit" disabled={busy}>
          {busy ? 'Signing in…' : 'Sign in'}
        </button>
        {error && (
          <p class="signin-error" role="alert">
            {error}
          </p>
        )}
      </form>
      <p class="signin-note">Read only. This site never changes anything in CrewLu Cloud.</p>
      <p class="signin-note">Forgot your password? Reset it in the Crewlu app.</p>
      <a class="signin-demo" href="?demo=1">See a demo</a>
    </main>
  )
}
```

```tsx
import { useMemo } from 'preact/hooks'
import type { AirportIndex } from '../data/airports'
import { buildLegs, type Leg } from '../engine/legs'
import { buildSchedule } from '../engine/normalize'
import { safeSnapshot } from '../engine/snapshot'
import type { Schedule } from '../engine/types'
import { prefers24Hour, viewerTimeZone } from '../time/zoned'
import { EmptyState } from './EmptyState'
import { Footer, type FooterProps } from './Footer'
import type { ScheduleHandle } from './hooks/useSchedule'
import { useNow } from './hooks/useNow'
import { useVisibilityCount } from './hooks/useVisibilityCount'
import { StatusScreen } from './StatusScreen'
import { Title } from './Title'

export interface ScheduleScreenProps {
  schedule: ScheduleHandle
  airports: AirportIndex
  demo: boolean
  onSignOut: () => void
}

/** Rebuilds the schedule when new rows arrive, and the snapshot every second. */
export function ScheduleScreen({ schedule, airports, demo, onSignOut }: ScheduleScreenProps) {
  const now = useNow()
  const celebrateKey = useVisibilityCount()
  const viewerTz = useMemo(() => viewerTimeZone(), [])
  const h24 = useMemo(() => prefers24Hour(), [])
  const built = useMemo<{ s: Schedule; legs: Leg[] } | null>(() => {
    if (!schedule.raw) return null
    try {
      const s = buildSchedule(schedule.raw, Date.now())
      return { s, legs: buildLegs(s) }
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('Could not read the schedule', err)
      return null
    }
  }, [schedule.raw])

  const footer: FooterProps = {
    now,
    lastSuccessAt: schedule.lastSuccessAt,
    newestUpdatedAt: schedule.raw?.newestUpdatedAt ?? null,
    problem: schedule.problem,
    refreshing: schedule.refreshing,
    onRefresh: schedule.refresh,
    onSignOut,
    signOutLabel: demo ? 'Exit demo' : 'Sign out',
    viewerTz,
    h24,
  }

  if (!built || built.s.empty) {
    return (
      <main class="screen">
        <Title text="CrewLuve" />
        {built?.s.empty && <EmptyState />}
        <Footer {...footer} />
      </main>
    )
  }

  const baseTz = airports.tz(built.s.pilot.base) ?? viewerTz
  const homeTz = airports.tz(built.s.pilot.effHome) ?? viewerTz
  const snap = safeSnapshot(built.s, built.legs, now, baseTz)
  return (
    <StatusScreen snap={snap} now={now} viewerTz={viewerTz} homeTz={homeTz} h24={h24} airports={airports} demo={demo}
      celebrateKey={celebrateKey} footer={footer} />
  )
}
```

Run: `npx vitest run tests/ui/ScheduleScreen.test.tsx`
Expected: PASS (3 tests).

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `src/ui/Root.tsx`**

```tsx
import { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from 'preact/hooks'
import { hasSession, onSignedOut, signOut } from '../auth/session'
import { localScheduleCache, noScheduleCache } from '../data/cache'
import { DEFAULT_SCENE, demoRawForScene, isDemoScene, type DemoScene } from '../data/demo'
import { fetchSchedule } from '../data/fetchSchedule'
import { supabase } from '../supabase'
import { useAirports } from './hooks/useAirports'
import { useSchedule } from './hooks/useSchedule'
import { ScheduleScreen } from './ScheduleScreen'
import { SignIn } from './SignIn'

/** ?demo=1 shows the sample pilot with no network calls; otherwise sign in to CrewLu Cloud. */
export function Root() {
  const params = new URLSearchParams(window.location.search)
  if (params.get('demo') === '1') {
    const scene = params.get('scene')
    return <DemoApp scene={isDemoScene(scene) ? scene : DEFAULT_SCENE} />
  }
  return <LiveApp />
}

function DemoApp({ scene }: { scene: DemoScene }) {
  const airports = useAirports()
  // Anchor the scene once, at page load, so the countdowns run in real time instead of resetting on refresh.
  const anchor = useMemo(() => Date.now(), [])
  const schedule = useSchedule({
    load: async () => ({ ...demoRawForScene(scene, anchor), fetchedAt: Date.now() }),
    cache: noScheduleCache,
  })
  if (!airports) return null
  return (
    <ScheduleScreen schedule={schedule} airports={airports} demo onSignOut={() => { window.location.href = window.location.pathname }} />
  )
}

function LiveApp() {
  const [phase, setPhase] = useState<'checking' | 'signedOut' | 'signedIn'>('checking')
  useEffect(() => {
    let alive = true
    void hasSession().then((ok) => {
      if (alive) setPhase(ok ? 'signedIn' : 'signedOut')
    })
    const unsubscribe = onSignedOut(() => {
      localScheduleCache().clear()
      setPhase('signedOut')
    })
    return () => {
      alive = false
      unsubscribe()
    }
  }, [])
  if (phase === 'checking') return null
  if (phase === 'signedOut') return <SignIn onSignedIn={() => setPhase('signedIn')} />
  return <LiveSchedule onSignedOut={() => setPhase('signedOut')} />
}

function LiveSchedule({ onSignedOut: finish }: { onSignedOut: () => void }) {
  const airports = useAirports()
  const cache = useMemo(() => localScheduleCache(), [])
  const endSession = async () => {
    await signOut()
    cache.clear()
    finish()
  }
  const schedule = useSchedule({
    load: () => fetchSchedule(supabase, Date.now()),
    cache,
    onAuthError: () => void endSession(),
  })
  if (!airports) return null
  return (
    <ScheduleScreen
      schedule={schedule}
      airports={airports}
      demo={false}
      onSignOut={() => {
        if (window.confirm('Sign out on this device?')) void endSession()
      }}
    />
  )
}
```

### Task 32: Visual check against the Crewluve app

The user judges by eye. This task makes the site look like the app before anyone sees real data, then confirms real data with the user.

The reference screenshots are in `~/Dev/App Photos for app store/Crewluve app Photos/`:
- `Crewluve in flight.png`
- `Crewluve Layover.png`
- `ipad Back at home.png`

The screenshots are slightly older than the current app, so these spec details win where they differ:
- no pronouns
- seconds only in the last hour
- the new footer

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/styles/tokens.css`, `src/styles/cards.css` (tuning only)
- Modify: `.claude/launch.json` (adds the production preview), `vite.config.ts` (preview `strictPort`, and the CSP if Step 5 tightens it), `tests/viteConfig.test.ts` (only if the CSP changes)

- [ ] **Step 1: Open the dev server in the browser pane**

Use the `preview_start` tool with `name: "crewluve"`. `.claude/launch.json` runs `npm run dev` on port 5180.
Then use `resize_window` with `width: 390, height: 844`, the iPhone size from the spec.

- [ ] **Step 2: Screenshot every demo scene**

For each scene in this list, navigate to `http://localhost:5180/?demo=1&scene=<scene>` and take a screenshot:
- `flying`, `turn`, `base`, `layover`, `delayed`, `last-leg`
- `driving-to-work`, `driving-home`, `commuting-home`, `home`, `reserve`, `training`

Expected:
- Each shows "Where's Alex?", the demo banner, the sentence from Tasks 15–16, and the right card (Back Home In / Leaves Home In / Released In).
- `last-leg` shows the red countdown.
- `home` shows the been-home pill.

The pane may run as a hidden tab, which pauses animation frames, so the celebration figure can look frozen. That's expected.

- [ ] **Step 3: Compare against the app and tune the CSS**

Open the three reference screenshots with the Read tool and compare them side by side with `flying`, `layover`, and `home`. Check:

- **Title:** centered, bold, the same size relative to the screen.
- **Narrative card:**
  - icon column width and 14 px gap
  - text size and line height
  - the ring hugs the icon
  - bold city names
  - the countdown in the status color
- **Countdown card:**
  - title row left-aligned with the house icon
  - a big rounded number with smaller black units, centered
  - the gray subtitle centered
  - 24 px padding
- **Card surfaces:** a light gray glass look on white, with a soft shadow and 20 px corners. Change only `--glass-fill`, `--glass-edge`, `--glass-rim` and `--glass-shadow` in `tokens.css` until it matches.
- **Spacing:** 24 px between cards and 16 px side margins.
- **Footer:** small gray rows centered between two round buttons.

Make one change at a time and re-screenshot. Don't touch layout files beyond `tokens.css` and `cards.css`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Check dark mode, the desktop width, and the sign-in screen**

- `resize_window` with `colorScheme: "dark"`, then screenshot `flying` and `home`. Expect a black background, light text, the lighter UPS brown (#E09E33), and readable glass.
  - Also check `commuting-home`. Many airline brand colors are dark: Delta is about 1.2:1 against a dark card, and American about 1.4:1, so a jumpseat's icon and bold countdown can be hard to read.
  - The app has the same problem, because only UPS changes in dark mode. Show the user and ask before changing anything (decision #2 is to copy the app).
- `resize_window` with `preset: "desktop"`, then screenshot `flying`. Expect one centered column no wider than 480 px.
- Navigate to `http://localhost:5180/`. The pane has no CrewLu Cloud session, so expect the sign-in screen: heart artwork, "CrewLuve", the form, the read-only note, and the "See a demo" link.
- Set `colorScheme: "light"` again.

- [ ] **Step 5: Check the production build under its CSP**

The dev server runs without the CSP, so check the built site. That's what GitHub Pages will serve.

1. Add a second configuration to `.claude/launch.json`:

   ```json
   { "name": "crewluve-preview", "runtimeExecutable": "npm", "runtimeArgs": ["run", "preview"], "port": 5181 }
   ```

   In `vite.config.ts`, change `preview` to `{ port: 5181, strictPort: true }` so the preview never drifts to another port.
2. Run `npm run build`, then `preview_start` with `name: "crewluve-preview"`.
3. Visit these pages. After each one, run `read_console_messages` with `onlyErrors: true`. Expect no "Content Security Policy" or "Refused to" errors.
   - `http://localhost:5181/?demo=1&scene=flying`
   - a scene with the celebration hearts (a countdown under 24 hours, such as `last-leg`)
   - `http://localhost:5181/` (sign-in)
4. Try a stricter policy:
   - Remove `'unsafe-inline'` from `style-src` in `vite.config.ts` and rebuild.
   - Restart the preview (`preview_stop`, then `preview_start`) and repeat step 3.
   - Also look at the progress ring and the hearts, whose colors come from `style` props. Preact sets those through the DOM style object, which the CSP allows, so this should work.

   If there are no violations and nothing looks different, keep the stricter policy:
   - Add a test to `tests/viteConfig.test.ts` that the policy contains no `'unsafe-inline'`.
   - Update the CSP block in spec §9.
   - Add a row to the spec's §12 decisions log.

   If there's any violation, put `'unsafe-inline'` back and say why in the comment above `CSP`.

- [ ] **Step 6: Show the user and take their notes**

Show the user the `flying`, `layover`, `last-leg`, `home` and sign-in screenshots next to the app screenshots. Ask whether anything looks off. Expect a round or two of nudges (brightness, spacing). Apply each in `tokens.css` or `cards.css`, re-screenshot, and show them again.

- [ ] **Step 7: Real data, with the user**

Ask the user to sign in at `http://localhost:5181/` in the browser pane, typing their own CrewLu Cloud email and password. **Never type credentials for them.**

This is the production build, so it also proves the CSP lets sign-in and the data fetch through.
- Confirm that the sentence, countdown and footer match where they are now and when they're next home.
- Run `read_console_messages` with `onlyErrors: true` and check for CSP or network errors.
- If anything is wrong:
  1. Write down the moment (status, time, what was expected).
  2. Add a failing engine test that reproduces it with `RawBuilder`.
  3. Fix it test-first.
  4. Rebuild, restart the preview, and re-check. The session stays in that origin's storage, so the user stays signed in.
- Sign out with the footer button afterward if the user wants.

- [ ] **Step 8: Reset the pane and run the gates**

Use `resize_window` with `preset: "desktop"`, and stop the preview server with `preview_stop`.
Run: `npx vitest run && npx tsc --noEmit && npm run build`
Expected: all green.

### Task 33: Deploy pipeline (ask before publishing anything)

**Files:**
- Create: `.github/workflows/deploy-pages.yml`, `.nvmrc`, `public/CNAME`, `docs/DEPLOY.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write `.github/workflows/deploy-pages.yml`**

This is the globe's workflow with two changes: a test step, and the Node version read from `.nvmrc` (Step 2).

```yaml
name: Deploy to GitHub Pages

# Tests and builds on every push to main, then publishes dist/ to Pages
# (custom domain crewluve.crewlu.net). Same pattern as crewlu-globe.
on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read
  pages: write
  id-token: write

# Never cancel an in-progress production deploy.
concurrency:
  group: pages
  cancel-in-progress: false

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version-file: .nvmrc
          cache: npm
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm test
      - run: npm run build
      - uses: actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3
        with:
          path: dist

  deploy:
    needs: build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    environment:
      name: github-pages
    # GitHub Pages often fails the first deploy with "Deployment failed, try again later"
    # and succeeds on a retry (seen on crewlu-globe), so try up to 3 times.
    steps:
      - id: a1
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v4
        continue-on-error: true
      - id: a2
        if: steps.a1.outcome != 'success'
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v4
        continue-on-error: true
      - id: a3
        if: steps.a1.outcome != 'success' && steps.a2.outcome != 'success'
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v4
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `public/CNAME` and `.nvmrc`**

`public/CNAME`:

```
crewluve.crewlu.net
```

`.nvmrc` holds one line. Node 24 is the current LTS, supported until April 2028. supabase-js 2.117 needs Node 22 or later, which `engines` in package.json also says.

```
24
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `docs/DEPLOY.md`**

````markdown
# Deploying crewluve.crewlu.net

The site is static. GitHub Actions tests and builds it on every push to `main` and publishes `dist/` to GitHub Pages.
No secrets are needed; the CrewLu Cloud publishable key is public by design.

Do these steps once, with the user. **Every step that publishes code or touches DNS needs the user's go-ahead.**

## 1. Create the GitHub repo and push

```bash
gh repo create skimmer747/crewluve-web --public --source . --remote origin --push
```

## 2. Turn on Pages

1. In GitHub, go to skimmer747/crewluve-web → Settings → Pages → Build and deployment.
2. Set Source to **GitHub Actions**.
3. If the "Deploy to GitHub Pages" run from step 1 started before Pages was on, re-run it from the Actions tab.

## 3. Point crewluve.crewlu.net at GitHub Pages

In the DNS for crewlu.net, add:

| Type  | Name     | Target                | TTL  |
|-------|----------|-----------------------|------|
| CNAME | crewluve | skimmer747.github.io. | Auto |

crewlu.net's CAA record already allows letsencrypt.org.

## 4. Custom domain and HTTPS

1. In Settings → Pages → Custom domain, enter `crewluve.crewlu.net` and save.
2. Wait for "DNS check successful", then tick **Enforce HTTPS**.
3. If the certificate never arrives: remove the custom domain, wait about a minute, then add it again. This was the globe's fix on 2026-06-22.

## 5. Verify

```bash
curl -sI "https://crewluve.crewlu.net/?cb=$RANDOM" | head -1
curl -s "https://crewluve.crewlu.net/?cb=$RANDOM" | grep -c Content-Security-Policy
```

Expect `HTTP/2 200` and `1`.

Then open https://crewluve.crewlu.net/?demo=1 on a phone, and sign in with a real CrewLu Cloud account.

## Later deploys

Push to `main`. The workflow tests, builds and deploys, retrying the Pages step up to three times.

Linking the site from crewlu.net/crewluve/ is a separate change in the CrewLu-Website repo. Ask first.
````

- [ ] **Step 6: Stop and ask the user**

Summarize what's ready. Ask whether to go ahead with `docs/DEPLOY.md` step 1, which creates the **public** GitHub repo and pushes. Ask separately before each later outward-facing step (Pages settings, DNS, HTTPS). Don't continue without a clear yes for each.

## Done when

- `npx vitest run`, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` are all green.
- Every demo scene matches the app's look, and the user has signed off on the screenshots (Task 32).
- The user has seen their own real status and countdown on localhost and agrees they're right.
- The deploy pipeline is committed. Publishing waits for the user (Task 33).
